「布里恩要的是事实，老爷子，不是讲演。」希斯说。这位奈约德叛军首领佝偻的身形从一群比他高的汉子中间挤过来，站到克拉夫特身旁。「简单说吧，布里恩，你的计划成功了。克拉夫特把你的口信转给了我——我一听到就折返，到他的舰上同他碰了头。特尔特死了，我很遗憾——可他找到了我们要找的东西。他那份发现放射性痕迹的报告，我没法置之不理。你的女朋友带着那具开了颅的尸体赶到时，我也正好到了，我们一起把那颅骨里的绿色水蛭细细看了个够。她对那东西的解释，说得通，分量也足。我们已经在实施登陆的时候，接到了你的通话，说马格特高塔里存放着东西。那之后，不过就是循着踪迹——还有你安下的发报机。」

「可午夜的那些爆炸呢？」布里恩插话，「我听见了！」

「就是要让你听见，」希斯笑道，「不光是你，还有这洞里的马格特。我们估摸着他们手里有武器、这洞防守森严。于是午夜时分，我们朝洞口投下几枚大型化学炸药炸弹。威力足够干掉岗哨，又不致把洞顶掀塌。我们还指望，更深处的马格特会弃守岗位，或者被想象出来的辐射吓退。他们果然照办。这一着灵极了。我们悄没声地摸进来，打他们一个措手不及。扫荡得干干净净——抓不到的，格杀勿论。」

「那些投敌的跃迁空间技术员里，还有一个活着，」克拉夫特说，「他向我们讲了你们两位拦停那些射向奈约德的炸弹的事。」

在场的奈约德人，没有一个能在他的话上再添什么，连惯常讥诮的希斯也不能。可布里恩能共情他们的感受——那份如释重负与欢欣交聚的暖意。这种感觉，他一生不会忘记。

===Original===

“There is no more war,” Brion translated for Ulv, knowing that the Disan had understood nothing of the explanation. As he said it, he realized that there was one glaring error in the story.

“You couldn’t have done it,” Brion said. “You landed on this planet *before* you had my message about the tower. That means you still expected the magter to be sending their bombs to Nyjord﻿—and you made the landings in spite of this knowledge.”

“Of course,” Professor Krafft said, astonished at Brion’s lack of understanding. “What else could we do? The magter are sick!”

Hys laughed aloud at Brion’s baffled expression. “You have to understand Nyjord psychology,” he said. “When it was a matter of war and killing, my planet could never agree on an intelligent course. War is so alien to our philosophy that it couldn’t even be considered correctly. That’s the trouble with being a vegetable eater in a galaxy of carnivores. You’re easy prey for the first one that lands on your back. Any other planet would have jumped on the magter with both feet and shaken the bombs out of them. We fumbled it so long it almost got both worlds killed. Your mind-parasite drew us back from the brink.”

===Chinese===

「不再有战争了。」布里恩为乌尔夫转译道，他知道这番解释那迪斯人一个字也没听懂。话一出口他才发觉，这个故事里有一处扎眼的破绽。

「你们不可能做到的，」布里恩说，「你们在这颗行星上登陆，是在我那通关于高塔的口信*之前*。那就是说，你们当时仍料定马格特会把炸弹射向奈约德——而你们明知这一点，还是登陆了。」

「那当然，」克拉夫特教授说，对布里恩的懵然不解深感诧异，「不然还能怎么办？马格特们病了！」

希斯瞧着布里恩一脸困惑，放声大笑。「你得明白奈约德人的心理，」他说，「一碰到战争和杀戮的事，我们行星就永远商定不出一条明智的路数。战争跟我们的哲学太格格不入，连对它做一番正确的考量都办不到。这就是在肉食动物的银河系里当食草动物的难处——谁头一个扑上你的背，你就成了谁的现成猎物。换了任何一颗别的行星，早就双脚一齐跳到马格特身上，把炸弹从他们身上抖出来了。我们磕磕绊绊磨蹭了那么久，险些把两个世界都搭进去。你那脑寄生体，把我们从悬崖边上拉了回来。」

===Original===

“I don’t understand,” Brion said.

“A simple matter of definition. Before you came we had no way to deal with the magter here on Dis. They really were alien to us. Nothing they did made sense﻿—and nothing we did seemed to have the slightest effect on them. But you discovered that they were *sick*, and that’s something we know how to handle. We’re united again; my rebel army was instantly absorbed into the rest of the Nyjord forces by mutual agreement. Doctors and nurses are on the way here now. Plans were put under way to evacuate what part of the population we could until the bombs were found. The planet is united again, and working hard.”

“Because the magter are sick, infected by a destructive life form?” Brion asked.

===Chinese===

「我不明白。」布里恩说。

「无非是定义上的一桩简单事。你们来之前，我们对迪斯这里的马格特毫无办法。他们对我们是真正的异类。他们的所作所为没有一样讲得通——而我们的所作所为，对他们似乎没有丝毫影响。可你发现了，他们是*病了*——而这正是我们懂得处置的事情。我们重新团结起来了；我的叛军经双方同意，即刻并入了奈约德部队的其余部分。医生和护士此刻已在来这儿的路上。在炸弹找到之前尽可能疏散居民的方案也已着手实行。这颗行星重新团结起来，正在发奋苦干。」

「因为马格特病了，被一种毁灭性的生命形态感染了？」布里恩问。

===Original===

“Exactly so,” Professor Krafft said. “We are civilized, after all. You can’t expect us to fight a war﻿—and you surely can’t expect us to ignore the plight of sick neighbors?”

“No﻿ ﻿… you surely can’t,” Brion said, sitting down heavily. He looked at Ulv, to whom the speech had been incomprehensible. Beyond him, Hys wore his most cynical expression as he considered the frailties of his people.

“Hys,” Brion called out, “you translate all that into Disan and explain to Ulv. I wouldn’t dare.”

===Chinese===

「正是如此，」克拉夫特教授说，「我们毕竟是文明人。总不能指望我们去打一场战争——更不能指望我们对患病近邻的困厄坐视不理吧？」

「不……更不能。」布里恩说着，沉沉坐了下去。他看向乌尔夫——那番话对他是全然的天书。再往外，希斯脸上挂着他那最讥诮的神情，正掂量着自己族人的种种软弱。

「希斯，」布里恩喊道，「这些都由你翻成迪斯语，讲给乌尔夫听吧。我可不敢。」

## XIX

===Original===
Dis was a floating golden ball, looking like a schoolroom globe in space. No clouds obscured its surface, and from this distance it seemed warm and attractive set against the cold darkness. Brion almost wished he were back there now, as he sat shivering inside the heavy coat. He wondered how long it would be before his confused body-temperature controls decided to turn off the summer adjustment. He hoped it wouldn’t be as sudden or as drastic as turning it on had been.

Delicate as a dream, Lea’s reflection swam in space next to the planet. She had come up quietly behind him in the spaceship’s corridor, only her gentle breath and mirrored face telling him she was there. He turned quickly and took her hands in his.

===Chinese===
迪斯成了一颗悬浮的金球，在太空中望去，像一架教室里用的地球仪。没有云层遮蔽它的表面，隔着这段距离，映衬着寒冷的黑暗，它显得温暖而诱人。布里恩裹在厚重的外衣里坐在那里发抖，此刻几乎倒希望自己还回到那上面去。他不知道，自己那套被搅乱的体温调节机制还要过多久才会决定关掉夏季转换。他只希望，那不会像当初开启时那样突然而剧烈。

轻盈如一场梦，莉娅的倒影在行星旁的太空中浮动。她从飞船的走廊里悄然走到他身后，只有她轻柔的呼吸和映在舷窗上的脸告诉他她在那里。他霍然转身，把她的双手握进掌心。

===Original===
“You’re looking infinitely better,” he said.

“Well, I should,” she said, pushing back her hair in an unconscious gesture with her hand. “I’ve been doing nothing but lying in the ship’s hospital, while you were having such a fine time this last week. Rushing around down there shooting all the magter.”

“Just gassing them,” he told her. “The Nyjorders can’t bring themselves to kill any more, even if it does raise their own casualty rate. In fact, they are having difficulty restraining the Disans led by Ulv, who are happily killing any magter they see as being pure *umedvirk*.”

“What will they do when they have all those frothing magter madmen?”

“They don’t know yet,” he said. “They won’t really know until they see what an adult magter is like with his brain-parasite dead and gone. They’re having better luck with the children. If they catch them early enough, the parasite can be destroyed before it has done too much damage.”

===Chinese===
「你看上去好得不能再好了。」他说。

「那是当然，」她说着，抬手把头发向后拢了拢，全然是不自觉的动作。「我什么也没干，净躺在船上的医院里，你倒好，这一个星期在下面过得那么痛快。东奔西跑，见着马格特就开枪。」

「不过是给他们熏毒气罢了，」他告诉她，「奈约德人再也不忍心多杀了，哪怕这样会抬高他们自己的伤亡。事实上，他们正费劲拦着乌尔夫率领的那些迪斯人——那些人正起劲地杀掉每一个被他们视为纯种*乌梅德维尔克*（umedvirk）的马格特。」

「等他们把那些口吐白沫的马格特疯子全都抓到手，打算怎么办？」

「他们还不知道，」他说，「要等亲眼见过一个成年马格特在脑寄生体死绝之后是什么模样，他们才真正有数。孩子们那边运气要好些。只要发现得够早，寄生体还来不及造成太大损伤，就能把它消灭掉。」

===Original===
Lea shuddered delicately and let herself lean against him. “I’m not that sturdy yet; let’s sit down while we talk.” There was a couch opposite the viewport where they could sit and still see Dis.

“I hate to think of a magter deprived of his symbiote,” she said. “If his system can stand the shock, I imagine there will be nothing left except a brainless hulk. This is one series of experiments I don’t care to witness. I rest secure in the knowledge that the Nyjorders will find the most humane solution.”

“I’m sure they will,” Brion said.

“Now what about us?” she said disconcertingly, leaning back in his arms. “I must say you have the highest body temperature of anyone I have ever touched. It’s positively exciting.”

This jarred Brion even more. He didn’t have her ability to put past horrors out of the mind by substituting present pleasures. “Well, just what about us?” he said with masterful inappropriateness.

===Chinese===
莉娅微微打了个寒噤，顺势倚进他怀里。「我还没那么结实呢；我们坐下来谈吧。」舷窗对面有一张长椅，坐在那里仍望得见迪斯。

「我真不愿去想一个被夺去共生体的马格特会怎样，」她说，「要是他的身体系统撑得住这场冲击，我想剩下的也不过是一具没了脑子的空壳。这一连串实验，我可不想亲眼看。好在奈约德人会找到最人道的解决办法，想到这一点我就放心了。」

「我确信他们会的。」布里恩说。

「那么，我们俩呢？」她倒在他怀里，这话问得人措手不及。「我得说一句，在我碰过的人里头，你的体温是最高的。实在叫人兴奋。」

这话让布里恩愈发狼狈。他可没有她那本事——拿眼下的欢愉把昔日的恐怖从心里顶出去。「呃——我们俩究竟怎么样？」他问道，那份从容自若全然用错了地方。

===Original===
She smiled as she leaned against him. “You weren’t as vague as that, the night in the hospital room. I seem to remember a few other things you said. And did. You can’t claim you’re completely indifferent to me, Brion Brandd. So I’m only asking you what any outspoken Anvharian girl would. Where do we go from here? Get married?”

There was a definite pleasure in holding her slight body in his arms and feeling her hair against his cheek. They both sensed it, and this awareness made his words sound that much more ugly.

“Lea—darling! You know how important you are to me—but you certainly realize that we could never get married.”

Her body stiffened and she tore herself away from him.

“Why, you great, fat, egotistical slab of meat! What do you mean by that? I like you, Lea, we have plenty of fun and games together, but surely you realize that you aren’t the kind of girl one takes home to mother!”

===Chinese===
她倚着他，笑了。「在医院病房里的那一晚，你可没有这么含糊。我似乎还记得你说过的另外一些话。还有做过的另外一些事。你总不能说对我全然无意吧，布里恩·布兰德。所以我不过是在问一句任何心直口快的安瓦尔姑娘都会问的话：我们往后怎么办？结婚吗？」

把她纤巧的身子搂在怀里，感觉到她的头发贴着自己的脸颊，这分明是一桩乐事。两人都觉察到了，而这份觉察，使他接下去的话显得愈发难听。

「莉娅——亲爱的！你知道你对我有多重要——可你一定也明白，我们永远结不了婚。」

她的身子一下子僵住，从他怀里猛然挣脱出去。

「好哇，你这块又肥又大、妄自尊大的肉板！你这话是什么意思？『我喜欢你，莉娅，我们一起玩得很快活，可你总该明白，你不是那种能带回家去见母亲的姑娘！』」

===Original===
“Lea, hold on,” he said. “You know better than to say a thing like that. What I said has nothing to do with how I feel towards you. But marriage means children, and you are biologist enough to know about Earth’s genes—”

“Intolerant yokel!” she cried, slapping his face. He didn’t move or attempt to stop her. “I expected better from you, with all your pretensions of understanding. But all you can think of are the horror stories about the worn-out genes of Earth. You’re the same as every other big, strapping bigot from the frontier planets. I know how you look down on our small size, our allergies and haemophilia and all the other weaknesses that have been bred back and preserved by the race. You hate—”

“But that’s not what I meant at all,” he interrupted, shocked, his voice drowning hers out. “Yours are the strong genes, the viable strains—*mine* are the deadly ones. A child of mine would kill itself and you in a natural birth, if it managed to live to term. You’re forgetting that you are the original *Homo sapiens*. I’m a recent mutation.”

Lea was frozen by his words. They revealed a truth she had known, but would never permit herself to consider.

===Chinese===
「莉娅，你等等，」他说，「你明事理，不该说这种话。我方才那句话，同我对你的感情毫不相干。可结婚就意味着孩子，而你是个生物学家，地球的基因是怎么回事，你不是不明白——」

「偏狭的乡巴佬！」她喊道，一巴掌打在他脸上。他没有动，也不曾试图阻拦。「我还指望你懂得多一些，亏你满口善解人意的调调。可你满脑子想的，全是那些地球基因耗损殆尽的恐怖故事。你跟边疆行星来的每一个高大壮硕的偏执鬼一模一样。我知道你们怎么看不起我们的小个子，看不起我们的过敏、血友病，还有这个种族一代代繁衍回来、保存下去的种种孱弱。你们恨——」

「可我根本就不是那个意思，」他惊愕地打断她，声音把她的话淹没了。「你的基因才是强韧的基因、有活力的品系——*我的*才是致命的。我的孩子就算能撑到足月，自然分娩时也会害死它自己、害死你。你忘了，你才是原初的*智人*（*Homo sapiens*）。我不过是一个晚近的突变。」

莉娅被他的话钉在了原地。那番话揭开了一个她其实一直知道、却从不许自己去想的真相。

===Original===
“Earth is home, the planet where mankind developed,” he said. “The last few thousand years you may have been breeding weaknesses back into the genetic pool. But that’s nothing compared to the hundred millions of years that it took to develop man. How many newborn babies live to be a year of age on Earth?”

“Why… almost all of them. A fraction of one percent die each year—I can’t recall exactly how many.”

“Earth is home,” he said again gently. “When men leave home they can adapt to different planets, but a price must be paid. A terrible price is in dead infants. The successful mutations live, the failures die. Natural selection is a brutally simple affair. When you look at me, you see a success. I have a sister—a success too. Yet my mother had six other children who died when they were still babies. And several others that never came to term. You know about these things, don’t you, Lea?”

===Chinese===
「地球是家园，是人类在其上演化成人的行星，」他说，「最近这几千年，你们或许一直在把孱弱繁衍回基因库里。可比起演化出人类所花的上亿年，这点岁月算不得什么。在地球上，有多少新生儿能活到一岁？」

「呃……几乎全都活得到。每年只有不到百分之一死去——确切数目我记不清了。」

「地球是家园，」他又温和地说了一遍，「人离开家园，能够去适应别的行星，但代价必须偿付。这代价可怕至极，是用死去的婴儿偿付的。成功的突变活下来，失败的死掉。自然选择是一桩简单得残酷的事。你看着我，看到的是一个成功者。我有一个妹妹——也是成功者。可我母亲另外生过六个孩子，个个还在襁褓中就夭折了。还有几个，压根没能足月。这些事你是知道的，对不对，莉娅？」

===Original===
“I know, I know…” she said sobbing into her hands. He held her now and she didn’t pull away. “I know it all as a biologist—but I am so awfully tired of being a biologist, and top of my class and a mental match for any man. When I think about you, I do it as a woman, and can’t admit any of this. I need someone, Brion, and I needed you so much because I loved you.” She paused and wiped her eyes. “You’re going home, aren’t you? Back to Anvhar. When?”

“I can’t wait too long,” he said, unhappily. “Aside from my personal wants, I find myself remembering that I’m a part of Anvhar. When you think of the number of people who suffered and died—or adapted—so that I could be sitting here now… well, it’s a little frightening. I suppose it doesn’t make sense logically that I should feel indebted to them. But I do. Anything I do now, or in the next few years, won’t be as important as getting back to Anvhar.”

“And I won’t be going back with you.” It was a flat statement the way she said it, not a question.

“No, you won’t be,” he said. “There is nothing on Anvhar for you.”

===Chinese===
「我知道，我都知道……」她把脸埋在双手里，抽泣着说。此刻他搂着她，她也没有挣开。「作为生物学家，这一切我全知道——可我实在厌倦透了当生物学家，厌倦透了当年级第一名、当才智不输任何男人的才女。想你的时候，我是作为一个女人在想你，没法承认这些。我需要一个人，布里恩，我那么需要你，因为我爱的是你。」她顿了顿，擦了擦眼睛。「你要回家了，是不是？回安瓦尔去。什么时候？」

「我拖不了太久，」他郁郁地说，「撇开我个人的意愿不谈，我总记起自己是安瓦尔的一部分。一想到有多少人受苦、死去——或者适应了下来——才换来我今天能坐在这里……嗯，这多少叫人心惊。照逻辑讲，我该觉得自己欠了他们什么，其实讲不通。可我确实觉得欠了。我现在做的任何事，或者今后几年做的任何事，都不会比回安瓦尔去更要紧。」

「而我不会跟你一起回去。」她说得平平的，是陈述，不是发问。

「是的，你不会，」他说，「安瓦尔没有属于你的东西。」

===Original===
Lea was looking out of the port at Dis and her eyes were dry now. “Way back in my deeply buried unconscious I think I knew it would end this way,” she said. “If you think your little lecture on the Origins of Man was a novelty, it wasn’t. It just reminded me of a number of things my glands had convinced me to forget. In a way, I envy you your weightlifter wife-to-be, and your happy kiddies. But not very much. Very early in life I resigned myself to the fact that there was no one on Earth I would care to marry. I always had these teenage dreams of a hero from space who would carry me off, and I guess I slipped you into the pattern without realizing it. I’m old enough now to face the fact that I like my work more than a banal marriage, and I’ll probably end up a frigid and virtuous old maid, with more degrees and titles than you have shot-putting records.”

As they looked through the port Dis began slowly to contract. Their ship drew away from it, heading towards Nyjord. They sat apart, without touching now. Leaving Dis meant leaving behind something they had shared. They had been strangers together there, on a strange world. For a brief time their lifelines had touched. That time was over now.

===Chinese===
莉娅望着舷窗外的迪斯，眼睛已经干了。「其实在我深深埋藏的潜意识里，我想我早就知道会是这个结局，」她说，「你要是以为你那篇『人类起源』的小讲座是什么新鲜东西，那可错了。它不过是让我想起了一串事——我的荷尔蒙早就劝我忘掉的那些事。从某种意义上说，我羡慕你那位举重运动员式的未婚妻，还有你那群快活的孩子。不过也羡慕不到哪里去。很小的时候我就认了命：地球上没有一个我愿意嫁的人。我少女时代总做些白日梦，梦想有个太空来的英雄把我带走，我多半是不知不觉就把你套进了那个模子。如今我年纪够大，足以面对这个事实：比起一段乏味的婚姻，我更喜欢我的工作；我很可能落得个冷淡而贞洁的老处女下场，到头来拿的学位和头衔，比你攒的铅球纪录还多。」

两人望着舷窗，迪斯开始缓缓收缩。他们的船正离它远去，朝奈约德驶去。他们分坐着，如今不再相触。离开迪斯，意味着把一件他们曾共享的东西留在身后。在那个陌生的世界上，他们曾是一对同在异乡的陌生人。有那么短短一段时光，他们的生命线彼此相触。如今那段时光结束了。

===Original===
“Don’t we look happy!” Hys said, shambling towards them.

“Fall dead and make me even happier then,” Lea snapped bitterly.

Hys ignored the acid tone of her words and sat down on the couch next to them. Since leaving command of his rebel Nyjord army he seemed much mellower. “Going to keep on working for the Cultural Relationships Foundation, Brion?” he asked. “You’re the kind of man we need.”

Brion’s eyes widened as the meaning of the last words penetrated. “Are you in the C.R.F.?”

“Field agent for Nyjord,” he said. “I hope you don’t think those helpless office types like Faussel or Mervv really represented us there? They just took notes and acted as a front and cover for the organization. Nyjord is a fine planet, but a gentle guiding hand behind the scenes is needed, to help them find their place in the galaxy before they are pulverized.”

“What’s your dirty game, Hys?” Lea asked, scowling. “I’ve had enough hints to suspect for a long time that there was more to the C.R.F. than the sweetness-and-light part I have seen. Are you people egomaniacs, power hungry or what?”

===Chinese===
「瞧咱们这模样，多快活！」希斯拖着脚步朝他们走来。

「那你干脆倒地死了，好叫我更高兴些。」莉娅刻薄地抢白道。

希斯不去理会她话里的酸味，在他们身旁的长椅上坐下。自从卸下他那支奈约德叛军的指挥权，他似乎随和多了。「打算继续替文化关系基金会做事吗，布里恩？」他问，「你正是我们需要的那种人。」

最后几个字的意思渗了进来，布里恩瞪大了眼睛。「你是文化关系基金会的人？」

「驻奈约德的外勤特工，」他说，「你总不会以为福塞尔、梅尔夫那种不顶用的办公室文员，真能在那儿代表我们吧？他们不过记记笔记，替组织充当门面和掩护。奈约德是颗好行星，可它需要幕后有一只温和的引路之手，帮它在被碾成齑粉之前，在银河系里找到自己的位置。」

「你们在玩什么肮脏把戏，希斯？」莉娅皱着眉问，「我得的暗示够多了，早就在疑心，文化关系基金会绝不只有我见到的那副和善光明的面目。你们这伙人到底是自大狂、权欲熏心，还是别的什么？」

===Original===
“That’s the first charge that would be leveled at us if our activities were publicly known,” Hys told her. “That’s why we do most of our work under cover. The best fact I can give you to counter the charge is *money*. Just where do you think we get the funds for an operation this size?” He smiled at their blank looks. “You’ll see the records later so there won’t be any doubt. The truth is that all our funds are donated by planets we have helped. Even a tiny percentage of a planetary income is large—add enough of them together and you have enough money to help other planets. And voluntary gratitude is a perfect test, if you stop to think about it. You can’t talk people into liking what you have done. They have to be convinced. There have always been people on C.R.F. worlds who knew about our work, and agreed with it enough to see that we are kept in funds.”

“Why are you telling me all this super-secret stuff,” Lea asked.

“Isn’t that obvious? We want you to keep on working for us. You can name whatever salary you like—as I’ve said, there is no shortage of ready cash.”

Hys glanced quickly at them both and delivered the clinching argument. “I hope Brion will go on working with us too. He is the kind of field agent we desperately need, and it is almost impossible to find.”

===Chinese===
「我们的活动一旦公开，人家头一条安在我们头上的就是这项罪名，」希斯对她说，「所以我们大部分工作都在暗中行事。要驳倒这项罪名，我能给出的最有力的凭据就是*钱*。你想，这么大规模的行动，我们的经费是打哪儿来的？」他瞧着两人茫然的神色，笑了。「回头你们会看到账目，到时候就不容怀疑了。事实是，我们的全部经费，都出自我们帮过的那些行星的捐助。一颗行星的收入哪怕只抽出极小的百分比，数目也极为可观——足够多笔加在一起，就有足够的钱去帮助别的行星。而且只要肯停下来想一想，自愿的感激是一种最完美的检验。你没法把人说得喜欢上你做成的事，他们必须自己被说服。在基金会开展工作的各个世界上，一直有人知道我们的工作，也足够赞同它，会设法保证我们的经费源源不绝。」

「那你为什么把这些绝密的事儿都讲给我听？」莉娅问。

「这还不明显？我们希望你继续为我们工作。你想要多少薪水，只管开口——我说过了，现款有的是。」

希斯飞快地瞥了他们俩一眼，抛出了那一锤定音的论据。「我希望布里恩也继续同我们共事。他正是我们迫切需要、却又几乎无处可寻的那种外勤特工。」

===Original===
“Just show me where to sign,” Lea said, and there was life in her voice once again.

“I wouldn’t exactly call it blackmail,” Brion smiled, “but I suppose if you people can juggle planetary psychologies, you must find that individuals can be pushed around like chessmen. Though you should realize that very little pushing is required this time.”

“Will you sign on?” Hys asked.

“I must go back to Anvhar,” Brion said, “but there really is no pressing hurry.”

“Earth,” said Lea, “is overpopulated enough as it is.”

===Chinese===
「只消指给我该在哪儿签字。」莉娅说，她的声音里重新有了生气。

「我倒不一定会把这叫做要挟，」布里恩笑道，「不过我想，既然你们这些人能摆弄整颗行星的心理，个体在你们手里自然也能像棋子似的被拨来拨去。只是你们也该明白，这一回几乎用不着怎么拨。」

「你肯签约入伙吗？」希斯问。

「我必须回安瓦尔去，」布里恩说，「不过倒真没有什么火烧眉毛的急事。」

「地球嘛，」莉娅说，「本来就已经够人满为患的了。」
